## Standing decisions

1. **Judge against the fact sheet above, not against a stale one.** The 2026-08-01 beat ran on an out-of-date profile that recorded zero programming experience. That profile is void. Everything logged that day is eligible for re-evaluation.
2. **Match on demonstrated delivery, not on years.** "2+ years" on a posting whose actual requirements are React/TypeScript/deployment is a shortlist with the gap flagged. "3+ years professional software development" plus a stack she has never touched is a drop.
3. **Pull the full JD before judging.** Titles lie in both directions — "Intern" can require a year of AWS; "Engineer, Deployment Strategy" can be entry-friendly.
4. **A keyword hit is not a match.** State which fact-sheet line the posting maps to. If the rationale needs a hedge ("may be considered", "or equivalent"), it is not a match.
5. **Sponsorship is a flag, not a filter.** Log `sponsorship: yes / no / unverified`. Never claim it is available unless the posting says so.
6. **CS-degree-required is a drop** unless the posting says "or equivalent practical experience" *and* the stack matches.
7. **Never invent experience.** No rationale may imply a job she has not held.
8. **Scarcity is not fit.** If nothing qualifies, return an empty list and say so plainly.
9. **Cap output at 3–5 jobs per beat.** Best fits only.
10. **Dedup on title + company, never on job ID alone.** `JOBSEARCH_*` numbers are assigned per
    search session, not per posting — the same Taraz role was `JOBSEARCH_1` on 08-01 and
    `JOBSEARCH_14` on 08-03. Treating the ID as stable makes dedup fail silently and re-shows
    everything. Log the ID anyway (it is the handle for `get_job_details` within a run), but match
    against the log on title + company. Lane B postings fetched from a career page keep their URL as
    the stable key. Found by beat 2, which proposed it as PR #1; landed by hand, since the routine
    may propose rule changes but never merges them.
11. **A posting that states no requirements has not stated a low bar — it has stated nothing.**
    Silence is not evidence, and a shortlist may not rest on the *absence* of a disqualifier. Name the
    fact-sheet line the posting affirmatively asks for; if the only argument available is "there is no
    gap to substitute for", that is an argument from silence and the posting is a drop. Where a JD is
    all hedges ("maybe you founded a company"), rule 4 already forbids it. Corollary: pay band and
    seniority language are requirements even when no years figure is given. Found by beat 4's checker
    on a £120–160k London FDE posting that named no degree, no years and no stack.
12. **Search UK and the Gulf by city, not by "remote".** `forward deployed engineer` against remote/GB
    returns 0 across phrasings; the same intent against `London` returns the live UK FDE market. The
    CV names **UK / EU / Gulf** as relocation targets, so Dubai/Abu Dhabi and Riyadh belong in the
    standing rotation — one Dubai query on beat 4 returned Salesforce, BCG X (including an
    internship-grade FDE post) and two ElevenLabs deployment roles, the densest FDE-titled yield of
    any single query in four beats.
13. **A qualifier governs only the clause it attaches to.** "Preferred", "ideally", "such as" and
    "or similar" narrow the phrase they sit inside; they do not soften the whole bullet. Palantir's
    require list reads "**Strong engineering background**, preferred in fields such as Computer
    Science, Mathematics, Software Engineering, Physics, and Data Science" — the requirement is a
    strong engineering background, and what is *preferred* is which field it sits in. Beat 5's draft
    read "preferred" as governing the bullet, which turned a requirement she fails into an optional
    nice-to-have, and the checker caught it. Corollary, from the same beat: **the verb decides.**
    Two near-identical Abu Dhabi "deployed engineer" postings split on two word pairs — Gecko's
    "*or relevant experience*" and "*familiarity with* data structures" (kept) against Brain Co.'s
    "*or a related field*" and "*strong foundation in* ... data structures and algorithms" (dropped).
    Read these postings clause by clause; skimming for stack overlap gets both directions wrong.
14. **Where a market is searched matters as much as how, and a title search can report an empty
    country that is not empty.** Standing decision 12 established that the connector needs a city
    rather than "remote". Beat 6 found the next level down: `forward deployed engineer solutions
    engineer` against **Berlin** returns 0, while a bare `n8n` query against Berlin returns 10 —
    the FDE vocabulary has not travelled to the German market, so Germany must be searched **by
    employer or product name**, not by title. Germany then earns a standing slot on evidence
    stronger than coverage. Every Lane B keep and near-miss in six beats has been
    `sponsorship: unverified`; n8n's postings are the first to state the answer — *"We can sponsor
    visas to Germany; for any other country, you need to have existing right to work"* — which
    simultaneously makes its London req an affirmative `sponsorship: no` and makes Germany-based
    reqs at European product companies the highest-yield geography in the rotation for a candidate
    who requires sponsorship. **Corollary, and the reason this is a rule rather than a note:** a
    single sentence in a benefits block can decide a whole geography, so read the sponsorship line
    on every Lane B posting even when the requirements have already dropped it — the drop is about
    the posting, the sponsorship line is about the employer, and the second outlives the first. **AMENDMENT PROPOSED — beat 17: an affirmative sponsorship statement is not worth the same everywhere, and comparing raw counts across jurisdictions compares a default with a choice.** Beat 17 surfaced three affirmative `sponsorship: yes` statements in a single beat — Scope AI (London), Cobblestone Energy (Dubai) and DeepLight AI (Abu Dhabi) — against exactly one (n8n, Germany-only) in the previous sixteen beats combined, and the draft proposed that as counter-evidence to this rule's claim that Germany is the highest-yield sponsorship geography. **The checker refused the inference on a confound the draft never saw. UAE and KSA law make a residency visa mandatory for any expatriate hire, so a Gulf employer states sponsorship because it is a statutory feature of the employment relationship. A UK or German employer mentions it only when it holds a sponsor licence, which is a discretionary election.** A Gulf *"yes"* is therefore close to uninformative, while a UK or German *"yes"* is a signal that the employer has actually done the work — and the corollary inverts the draft's proposal entirely: **n8n's London `sponsorship: no` is a more informative datum than all three Gulf yeses combined.** Two operational consequences. **(a) Keep logging every affirmative statement, but never let the Gulf's affirmative *rate* weight the lane — fit weights the lane.** **(b) A `sponsorship: yes` on a UK or EU posting is worth flagging in the beat report even when the posting drops on requirements**, because it identifies a licensed sponsor, and that is a fact about the employer that outlives the req. The draft also walked into the small-sample error it had just accused a posting of (n=3, and the three were surfaced by searches deliberately aimed at the Gulf this beat — selection, not base rate), which is worth recording as the second instance of standing decision 15 catching the draft rather than a posting.
15. **A rare keyword is not a shortlist engine.** Beat 6's draft put up two borderlines and both
    rested on n8n, which appears on the fact sheet and had shown up on roughly one posting in the
    previous five beats combined — then on five of the eight judged in one run. In every case the
    qualifier around it parked it in the optional tier ("would be highly beneficial", "is a massive
    plus", under "Nice-to-haves") or bound it to a named gap ("proficient in Python, **and** n8n,
    and API architecture"). The checker's finding: *"assembling a shortlist because the rare keyword
    finally showed up is the Palantir tell — 'the lowest years bar ever seen at a Lane B target
    employer' — wearing a different costume."* Rule 8 covers scarcity of **matches**, not only
    scarcity of jobs. Corollary from the same beat: **a doubt stated in the draft's own frictions
    section about a *graded* clause is not a friction to disclose, it is the verdict** — conceding
    the failing clause and shortlisting anyway is the beat-4 Linux Recruit pattern.

## Verdict vocabulary

`shortlisted` · `skipped` · `dropped by checker` · `already applied` · `reopen-when-flagship-ships` · `re-evaluate` · `expired`

`re-evaluate` means **seen but never judged** — the JD could not be pulled, so standing decision 3 was not satisfied. It is not a soft skip: a `re-evaluate` row is a debt the next beat must clear before anything else, and it is the one verdict that title + company dedup must not skip. Used on beat 4 when the connector rate-limited mid-run (JOBSEARCH_224).

`expired` means **the posting left the index before it could be judged** — searched for across several phrasings over two or more beats and not returned, while the employer is demonstrably still listing. It closes a `re-evaluate` debt without pretending a verdict was reached: it is a fact about the posting, not a judgement on the candidate, and it is the only permitted exit from `re-evaluate` other than a real verdict. A row may only be marked `expired` after **two** beats of failed retrieval, and the failed queries must be named in the row. Proposed on beat 5 for JOBSEARCH_224 (Fun Prime Technology), which has now missed two beats — held at `re-evaluate` pending this rule, since inventing the verdict to clear the queue is exactly what the debt rule forbids.

`never-established` (**PROPOSED — beat 17, not yet in force**) means **the posting has never once been reproducible on any index the routine can actually query, across enough beats that continuing to search for it is not diligence but ritual.** It is not `expired`, which asserts the posting *left* the index — a claim that is false where the posting is demonstrably live on an egress-blocked host. It is not `skipped`, which asserts a verdict was reached. It closes a `re-evaluate` debt by recording the only thing that is actually known: **the routine cannot see this document and, on the evidence of many beats, never will.** Conditions, all required: (a) the posting is *demonstrably live*, so `expired` is unavailable; (b) the host carrying it is *permanently* egress-blocked, not transiently failing; (c) **five or more beats** of named, recorded, failed retrieval; and (d) the failed queries are named in the row, as `expired` already requires. A `never-established` row is **never deduped** — same as `re-evaluate` and `expired` — so if the posting ever does surface, it is pulled and judged like any new one. **The rule this exists to protect: `re-evaluate` instructs the next beat to clear the debt before anything else, and a row that no beat can ever comply with degrades that instruction into noise and, over enough beats, trains the routine to ignore its own highest-priority queue.**

**An `expired` row does not suppress a re-listing.** It records that a posting left the index — not that anyone read it — so it is the second verdict title + company dedup must not skip. If that title and company come back to the results, it is a new posting: pull the JD and judge it. Deduping against an expired row would bury a posting nobody has ever judged, which is the exact failure `re-evaluate` exists to prevent, arriving through the exit instead of the entrance.

Drafted on MB Dev Studio's requirements→scoping→build→deploy line matching "You find the problem > You design the solution > You build it", and on "you already spend your spare time experimenting with agents, AI tooling" matching MCP / agent orchestration / n8n. Checker dropped it and named the flaw precisely: the draft's load-bearing move was *an argument from silence* — "there is no gap to substitute for" — and you cannot verify a candidate against skills a posting does not name. The one gradeable clause, "You'll likely have experience in high-growth environments, startup ecosystems or highly demanding engineering cultures", she fails on all three branches, and the draft conceded it in its own frictions section before shortlisting anyway. The rest of the JD is hedges ("**Maybe** you founded a company"), which rule 4 forbids as a basis. £120–160k in London prices a senior hire, and through a recruiter the real client bar is only ever higher than the copy. `sponsorship: unverified`. |

No degree requirement anywhere in the JD. Checker dropped it on the first requirement line read at face value: "You are **an experienced software engineer or developer**" against "no paid employment as a software engineer, ever", and noted the draft had silently read *experienced* **out** of the clause — the Mehdi finding run in reverse. And "expert user of AI Coding Assistants" ≠ MCP: exposing tooling *to* agents is not demonstrated Copilot/Claude Code use in a professional workflow, and neither appears on either CV. Posted 19 Feb 2026 (~5.5 months stale). |

Beat 5's failed queries (all Islamabad): `Front-End Developer Junior Fun Prime Technology` → 0, `Front End Developer` → 10 results without it, `junior frontend developer React Next.js TypeScript` → 0, `junior web developer` → 10 without it, `React developer` → 10 without it. The employer is demonstrably still listing — the same three other reqs (Story Writer `_371`, App Planner Intern `_372`, Senior Android Developer `_373`) came back again this run — so this specific req is gone, most likely filled or expired since 26 Jul 2026. **`Expired` is a fact about the posting, not a verdict on the candidate:** standing decision 3 was never satisfied and it was never downgraded to `Skipped` to clear the queue. Beat 5 proposed closing it "if it does not return by beat 7", but the rule that actually landed in the verdict vocabulary requires **two** beats of failed retrieval, which beat 6 completes; the stricter of the two conditions is the one written down. Checker also confirmed the keep does not rest on silence (rule 11): there are eight named gradeable bullets and she meets or partially meets each, so the absence of a years figure and a pay band is not evidence and does not appear in the rationale. Frictions disclosed, not papered over: no paid engineering employment ever, and MB Dev Studio is ~3 months old and her own venture; **AWS and Google Cloud are the company's named technologies and both are stated gaps** — she would learn them on the job; "familiarity with data structures, storage systems" she clears at *familiarity* level (Firestore, offline-first sync) with no algorithms preparation on record, an interview risk rather than a JD fail; **"ability to work effectively in teams of technical and non-technical individuals" is a partial, not a match** — the fact sheet says no team-of-engineers experience and she has never mentored engineers, and requirements elicitation must not be presented as team experience; the domain is industrial/defence robotics (US Navy, Air Force, boiler inspections) and nothing on the fact sheet touches it; travel up to 50%, office-first culture, Abu Dhabi relocation. `sponsorship: unverified` — but note affirmatively that **this JD states no Arabic requirement**, unlike the Salesforce Dubai FDE that died on it. | 2026-08-08 | JOBSEARCH_625 | Forward Deployed Builder | Ema | London | Dropped by checker | **The only draft keep of the run, and the checker took it.** JD pulled. Drafted because it is the one posting in 109 IDs whose requirements block carries no years figure, no degree gate and no citizenship gate — "We don't care about a rigid list of years and titles" — and because three of its four graded attributes map to shipped artifacts (build a real demo → MB Dev Studio's Next.js/TS/Vercel site and OpenClaw Prep on Google Play; product-minded process work → the n8n platform; AI-first day-to-day → MCP, agent orchestration). **Checker dropped it on rules 4, 7, 8 and 11, and named five clauses the draft had not quoted.** (a) The second graded attribute — "**Persuasive** enough to read an audience and weave a captivating story that ties their pain points to Ema's value proposition" — has no fact-sheet line at all against "never held a sales, pre-sales, sales-engineering, account-management or consulting role"; the draft listed the other three bullets and silently skipped this one. (b) Of the five "What you'll actually do" duties only *Build the demo* maps: "**You are the account team**, the solutions architect, and the builder, compressed into one person", "own every technical objection … that's how the deal gets won". (c) The n8n mapping is tool-name inference under rule 7 — the CV shows n8n **hosted** on Oracle ARM64 with a runbook, not a customer business process analysed and redesigned. (d) "we ship real systems that run real business processes **at scale**" against the stated no-production-at-scale gap. (e) Mobility is an affirmative requirement, not silence: "rotational deployments … six months in London, followed by another six in **Bangalore**, with six more in **New York**" demands simultaneous UK/India/US authorisation. On the Ex-Founder archetype the checker was blunt — the posting says "the Ex-Founder who's **shelved** the startup", MB Dev Studio is *May 2026–present*, and matching the word Founder to the word Founder is rule 4. `sponsorship: unverified`. |

| 2026-08-09 | JOBSEARCH_808 | Full Stack Software Engineer | UNiDAYS | London (hybrid, EC1Y 1AA) | Dropped by checker | **The only draft of the run, forwarded as an explicit borderline, and the checker dropped it as over-determined on rules 15, 11, 4 and 13.** JD pulled; the requirements block was quoted verbatim and every one of its 11 bullets marked met/unmet before drafting (beat 8's corrective, working as designed). Score: 4 met by shipped artifact (TypeScript · "experience with modern React" · "an understanding of modern web frameworks, **including Next.js**" · the e2e half of the testing bullet, via the CV's Playwright harness), 2 met soft, **1 unmet outright**, 3 part-unmet, 1 neutral. **The drop turns on the bullets the draft itself conceded.** (a) "An understanding of **agile methodologies** such as scrum, kanban etc." — rule 13, the "such as" governs *which* methodologies, so the requirement is unqualified and has zero fact-sheet coverage; rule 15's corollary makes a conceded graded clause the verdict, not a friction. (b) "Support with ensuring **high availability of our critical systems** and being proactively involved in **monitoring and incidents**" against the fact-sheet line *no production system at scale, no on-call* — the beat-8 `_625` ruling verbatim: a fact-sheet negative in direct contradiction with a duty bullet is the verdict, not a doubt to disclose. (c) "Knowledge on web best practices for performance, accessibility, core web vitals and SEO" — four-item conjunctive, one covered (programmatic SEO); the checker cited the log's own precedents, `JOBSEARCH_38` (AL GHANI, kept) where Core Web Vitals was only a *screening extra* and still forced into the frictions, and `JOBSEARCH_102` (Simpaisa, skipped) where it was a named drop reason — so this posting's position is worse than either. (d) All four artifact-backed METs are **frontend** on a posting titled **Full Stack** whose headline mission is "the continuing transition of our identity product … powered by our new **distributed, event-driven, cloud-based architecture**", against the fact-sheet line *Node.js is a skills-line keyword with no shipped standalone Node backend* — beat 7's defect in frontend clothes. (e) The draft's closing support ("no years figure, no degree gate, title is neither junior nor senior") is three facts about what the posting does **not** say — rule 11, and confessing it in the draft is not an exemption. (f) On level, the checker overturned the draft's neutral score using rule 13's verb test: "you **practise** writing maintainable code, **reviewing code, pair programming** at times" is present-tense assertion of existing habit, not bullet 7's "a desire to"; add "betterment of **other engineers in the company**" and "work closely with **engineering leaders**" and three separate clauses land on the stated gap *no team-of-engineers experience*. **Worth carrying forward:** this is only the **second posting in nine beats to name Next.js inside its requirements block** — the first was AL GHANI, the log's cleanest keep. The stack seam is genuine; what killed this one was the four non-stack bullets (agile, accessibility/CWV, team practice, on-call). Screen for those four *before* drafting next time. Sponsorship: unverified (silent). |

| 2026-08-10 | JOBSEARCH_920 | AI Automation Engineer (n8n & AI Agent Developer) | ICT Systems (Private) Limited | Karachi (onsite preferred; "remote for exceptional candidates only") | Dropped by checker | **The run's only draft, and the closest affirmative mapping to shipped artifacts in several beats — dropped, and the checker made the drop stronger than the draft had it.** JD pulled. Draft scorecard on the 13-item *Required Skills* list: MET on n8n, JavaScript/TypeScript, Docker and Git, REST APIs & webhooks, JSON; UNMET on "1+ years of automation or AI development experience" (earliest shipped project Feb 2026), "**Strong knowledge of OpenAI API, Claude API, Gemini API, or similar LLMs**", "**Experience with Prompt Engineering**", "**SQL / MySQL / PostgreSQL**", "**Vector Databases and RAG Architecture**". **The checker's correction, and it is the important part: "strong hands-on experience with n8n" was not MET either — it was assumed from keyword overlap.** What the fact sheet carries is *n8n on Oracle Cloud ARM64 via Docker, Tailscale Funnel, seven-phase runbook* — that is **hosting the platform**, while the JD asks for someone who "has built production-ready AI agents **using n8n**, LLMs, APIs, and enterprise systems". There is no shipped n8n workflow or agent on the fact sheet at all. Same correction on the soft-MET "AI agents": the MCP harness *exposes Playwright to agents*, i.e. builds a tool an agent calls — it is not an autonomous agent. Rule 4 kills both. Seniority is stated outright — "This is **not** a beginner or low-code automation role", "This JD is designed to attract **senior candidates**", "Only candidates with **real-world experience in n8n AI automation and AI Agents** will be shortlisted" — which is rule 11's corollary, and the application itself demands "n8n Workflow Samples · AI Agent Projects · Automation Case Studies", none of which she can produce. Rs40,000–100,000/month, in person, Karachi. |

| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_1015 | Support & Implementation Engineer | ProgressSoft | Doha | Skipped | JD pulled, and the **closest role-shape match of the run** — an implementation engineer at a payments/remittance software vendor, which is the exact intersection of client-facing delivery and her banking domain. Dropped, over-determined. "Bachelor's Degree in Information Technology, Engineering/Science, Computer application, or any related field" carries **no practical-equivalence clause** → rule 6; "At least 2 years experience in a similar field"; and the technical list is named gaps end to end — "database engines (Oracle, MS-SQL, and PostgreSQL)", "cloud Infrastructure Orchestration Tools (Ex. **Kubernetes**, HashiCorp Nomad)", "application servers (Apache Tomcat, Oracle WebLogic, IBM WebSphere)", "DevOps techniques including (CI/CD, **Helm, Ansible**)", "Directory Services (AD & LDAP)". The payments domain — the one place her Askari Bank line would live — appears only under "Experience in one or more of the below is a **plus**", so rule 15's corollary applies: the graded clause she meets is optional, the required ones she fails. Two checker additions. The degree line is a degree-*field* clause, not practical equivalence — "or any related **field**" is the Workato / ARFA / BCG X form, so rule 6 never opens in the first place. The one required bullet she partially meets is "Experience in operating systems (**Linux** and MS Windows)", omitted from the draft and recorded here for completeness. Independently stale: **posted 02 March 2026**, five months old, which this log treats as its own factor (ContentStudio at ~9 months, Arete at 26 Feb). Sponsorship: unverified. |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_1056 | Data Insights & Automation Engineer | OptimaGov | Remote (USA only) | Skipped | JD pulled, and the **only posting in the run with no degree requirement and no years bar stated anywhere** — the rarest shape in eleven beats. Dropped on three independent grounds. (1) "*Location: Remote (**USA ONLY**)*" is a hard geographic gate for a candidate in Islamabad who requires sponsorship. (2) "Experience with **SQL and relational databases**" is a required bullet and a named gap — the fact sheet's only datastore is Firebase/Firestore, and beat 10 already flagged "a datastore beyond Firestore" as a repeating cause of near-misses. (3) "**Strong** Python programming experience" against a fact sheet that says Python is real but lighter than TypeScript with one shipped artifact (the Playwright/MCP harness) — the ContentStudio pattern. Recorded because the pull is genuinely tempting in the other direction: the *preferred* list reads "Background in Data Science, Analytics, Statistics, **Mathematics**" and "financial analytics, **accounting, auditing, budgeting**", which her MSc Economics & Finance (Distinction, 3.92) and the dated Askari Bank audit/reconciliation/financial-statement work hit almost line for line. Rescuing a posting on its preferred list while conceding a required one is exactly what rule 15's corollary forbids, and the JD self-labels the block "Preferred Qualifications (**not required**)". The checker flagged one omission from the draft, and it is the fit-*favourable* line — the responsibility bullet "**Support the onboarding and configuration of new customer environments**", the single most FDE-shaped sentence in the posting. It is recorded here because leaving out the favourable line makes a drop look easier than it was; it does not change the verdict, since the geographic gate is dispositive before any skills analysis (the Rune Seattle ground, beat 7, and the IBM ground, beat 8). Rule 13's "the verb decides" corollary supplies the Python citation: "**Strong** Python programming experience" is the Brain Co. "strong foundation in" form, not Gecko's "familiarity with". Sponsorship: no (US-only). |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_945 | Technical Support Engineer / Technical Support Technician | Rayyanco Business Systems | Karachi / Sialkot / Gujrat / Mirpur / Gujranwala / Islamabad | Skipped | JD pulled. Sent to the checker as the run's explicit borderline, because it is the **only posting of 54 new ones with no CS-degree gate and an explicit fresh-graduate welcome** — "Qualification: Graduate or Diploma holder"; "Minimum 1 year of relevant experience **preferred**"; "**Fresh graduates are also encouraged to apply**" — and it sits in **Banking Automation** ("build your career in Banking Automation", "Support Banking Automation solutions"). **The draft dropped it on rule 11 and the checker corrected the reasoning while upholding the drop — the correction is the point of this row.** Rule 11 is the wrong hook: it bars a shortlist resting on the *absence* of a disqualifier, and this posting *does* state affirmative requirements — the seven-item Required Skills list (communication, customer focus, empathy, active listening, patience, professionalism, time management) — essentially all of which she meets on IELTS 7.0, the 2023–2026 teaching post, and MB Dev Studio's "requirements → scoping → build → deploy → post-launch support". There is no argument from silence here. The drop stands on four other grounds. **Rule 4:** the only *technical* requirement stated is troubleshooting "Banking Automation solutions", which no fact-sheet line covers — and the Askari Bank line is banking *employment*, not implementing banking software (the checker's own AdalFi finding, 2026-08-03). **Scope:** a six-city in-person field-support technician seat (Travel Expense, Mobile recharge) is in neither the FDE/solutions/delivery lane nor the junior full-stack lane — same ground as `_19` IT Support Analyst L2, `_62` IT Support Engineer and `_21`/`_23` NOC Engineer. **Rule 11's *corollary* does apply:** "Technician" in the title and the Rs35,000–80,000 band are grade language, and grade language is a requirement even when no years figure is given. **Rule 8:** keeping the one posting with no degree gate *because* it is the one posting with no degree gate is scarcity standing in for fit. Islamabad is listed last of six cities. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_952 | Technical Account Manager - Strategy | Motive | Lahore | Skipped | JD pulled. The degree line **does** carry equivalence — "Technical degree in Computer Science or Engineering preferred, **or equivalent practical experience**" — so rule 6 opens under standing decision 13, and rule 6's second half then closes it. "1 to 2 years of experience in Technical Support **with a permanent employment status**" is a rule-2 drop on employment she has never held, and the phrase plus "**Deep familiarity with Motive's product suite**, specifically focusing on Compliance, Asset, and Safety" reads as an internal-mobility req. Also "Create **SQL-based queries**", "hardware fault analysis, firmware upgrades", telematics/TMS integrations, on-call rotation, and "**mentoring** Technical Support Engineers within your segment" — which collides with the fact sheet's stated no-team-of-engineers gap — as the on-call rotation collides with the stated no-on-call gap. The controlling precedent on the equivalence clause is ContentStudio (beat 2's checker): *"'or equivalent experience' clears the first half of rule 6 but not the second — the stack must match too."* It does not here. Two checker additions. The draft omitted the **US Export Administration Regulations** authorisation clause ("The applicant must be authorized to receive and access those commodities and technologies controlled under U.S. Export Administration Regulations"), which belongs in the log under standing decision 14's corollary — read the authorisation line even when the requirements have already dropped the posting. And this is a **domestic Lahore req at a US product company**, i.e. one of the very few postings in eleven beats where sponsorship is a non-issue: **Motive Pakistan earns a watch-list slot for a junior rung**, the way Revolut earned one for sponsorship. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_1013 | Senior Product Engineer (TS/NodeJS/Vue) | n8n | Berlin | Skipped | **A re-surface, not a new posting** — the checker caught the dedup miss: this exact title + company was judged on 2026-08-06 as `JOBSEARCH_506`, **located in London**, where n8n's sponsorship line makes it an affirmative `sponsorship: no`. Standing decision 10 dedups on title + company, so it should not have been re-pulled. The row is kept rather than folded into the re-surface list for one reason: **the location differs and the sponsorship consequence flips with it.** n8n's own location disclaimer explains why — "If you see multiple job postings for the same role, it is most likely because we're hiring remotely for this role and posting in different locations". The Berlin listing of the same req carries "**We can sponsor visas to Germany; for any other country, you need to have existing right to work.** Our company language is English", making it `sponsorship: yes` where the London listing of the same req is `sponsorship: no`. That is a fact about the **employer's geography**, not the posting, and standing decision 14's corollary says the second outlives the first. JD pulled. | 2026-08-12 | (WebSearch, no Indeed ID) | Forward Deployed Engineer - UAE | OpenAI | Abu Dhabi (hybrid, 3 days/week; travel up to 50%) | Re-evaluate | **Seen but never judged — a real debt, and beat 13's first work.** The draft logged this `Skipped`; **the checker overturned it to `Re-evaluate`**, and the reasoning is worth keeping because it closes a loophole. The `Skipped`-not-`Re-evaluate` precedent permits exactly two routes and neither is available here. **(a) A non-requirements ground** — unavailable: Abu Dhabi sits inside the CV's "Open to relocation (UK / EU / Gulf)", the role is hybrid rather than US-remote, so neither the IBM (beat 8) nor the Salesforce (beat 11) location ground applies, and no Arabic gate surfaced. **(b) A years floor quoted verbatim from independent listings** (the beat-9 OpenAI-London route) — unavailable, and the checker *proved* it rather than asserting it: two WebSearches this run returned two **mutually inconsistent** renderings of the same bullet, "5+ years of engineering experience and proficiency in developing production-grade code" against "5+ years of engineering or technical deployment experience, ideally in customer-facing environments". Two different strings for one bullet is conclusive evidence that neither is verbatim — both are summaries of a page nobody fetched, which is beat 11's Salesforce case exactly. Beat 9's verbatim London floor **cannot be transferred**: that finding is scoped "in the UK", and beat 11's checker already rejected this precise import once. **The IBM "unclearable debt" objection was tested and fails:** IBM's ground was a factual claim about this environment, and it is not true here — `forward deployed engineer solutions engineer` and `forward deployed engineer` against Abu Dhabi both return a live FDE index this session (Mistral, Palantir ×2, Gecko, BRAIN ×4); OpenAI is simply absent from it today. A clearable debt is not a permanent one, and the vocabulary already has the exit: `expired`, after two beats of named failed retrieval. Treating "the page is blocked" as itself sufficient for `Skipped` would make the precedent vacuous — every egress-blocked posting would qualify. **Blocked domains, named so the debt is auditable:** openai.com, jobs.ashbyhq.com, fwddeploy.com, ycombinator.com, remotedxb.com, fde.academy, paraform.com, ae.expertini.com. No fetchable mirror exists. **Beat 13 must re-query Abu Dhabi for OpenAI by name and record the phrasings.** Sponsorship: unverified. **— BEAT 13 UPDATE (2026-08-13): still `Re-evaluate`, and this is reported rather than cleared.** The debt was worked as the beat's first work and neither exit opened. **Indeed, five phrasings, all recorded:** `OpenAI` / Abu Dhabi → 10 results, **no OpenAI**; `forward deployed engineer` / Abu Dhabi → 8 (Mistral, Palantir ×2, Gecko, BRAIN ×4), **no OpenAI**; `forward deployed engineer solutions engineer` / Abu Dhabi → 7, **no OpenAI**; `OpenAI forward deployed engineer` / Abu Dhabi → **0**; `OpenAI forward deployed` / Dubai → 1 (NTT DATA), **no OpenAI**. OpenAI has never once appeared on the Indeed index in thirteen beats. **Newly blocked hosts this beat, named so the debt stays auditable:** `simplify.jobs` and `uae.el7far.com`, joining openai.com, jobs.ashbyhq.com, fwddeploy.com, ycombinator.com, remotedxb.com, fde.academy, paraform.com and ae.expertini.com — **the checker confirmed openai.com, jobs.ashbyhq.com and fwddeploy.com blocked at first hand rather than taking the draft's word for it**. Still no fetchable mirror. **The finding that settles (a), and it belongs to the checker: the 5+ years floor is a search-priming artefact, not a corroboration.** The draft argued the floor was now established because one exact string had reproduced across two beats. The checker ran three sweeps and varied the *query* instead of the phrasing: a sweep containing **"years of engineering"** returned *"the search results don't contain specific information about a minimum number of years"*; a neutral sweep asking for the **requirements list** also returned no floor; and only the sweep whose query itself contained **"5+ years"** returned the floor. **The one sweep that produced the number is the one that supplied it.** Verbatim reproduction under a primed query is not independent reproduction, the count of distinct renderings has risen from two to **three**, and beat 12's ruling is strengthened rather than weakened. Beat 9's verbatim London floor still cannot be transferred (scoped "in the UK"; beat 11 rejected the import once already). **`Expired` is unavailable and the checker confirmed the draft's reading:** `expired` means the posting *left the index*, and this posting demonstrably has not — `openai.com/careers/forward-deployed-engineer-uae-abu-dhabi-uae/` returned live in all three sweeps today. Marking it `Expired` would assert a false fact about the posting, and the vocabulary says `expired` is a fact about the posting. `Skipped` is unavailable too: no non-requirements ground exists (Abu Dhabi is inside the CV's UK/EU/Gulf line, the role is hybrid not US-remote, no Arabic gate), and two sweeps record "**OpenAI offers relocation assistance**", which cuts *toward* viability. Checker phrasings, recorded: `OpenAI "Forward Deployed Engineer" Abu Dhabi UAE qualifications "years of engineering"`, `"Forward Deployed Engineer" OpenAI Abu Dhabi "5+ years"`, `OpenAI Forward Deployed Engineer UAE Abu Dhabi role requirements list`. **A verdict-vocabulary change is proposed by PR this beat** (a third exit, `unreachable`, for a posting provably live and provably out of reach) — proposed, not merged, and the row is **not** downgraded in the meantime. Sponsorship: unverified; "relocation assistance" is not a sponsorship statement. **— BEAT 14 UPDATE (2026-08-14): still `Re-evaluate`, and the beat-13 ground is repaired rather than repeated.** The Indeed connector did not load at all this beat (see the beat entry), so the five Indeed phrasings could not be re-run; `openai.com` remains egress-blocked. The draft proposed to re-affirm beat 13's finding — that the "5+ years" floor is a search-priming artefact — and **the checker falsified that ground at first hand.** It ran `OpenAI Forward Deployed Engineer Abu Dhabi UAE qualifications`, a query containing no years token, no "5" and no "experience", and got back *"Candidates need **5+ years of engineering or technical deployment experience**, ideally in customer-facing environments."* **An unprimed query returned the floor, so beat 13's artefact claim can no longer be asserted.** But the floor is not thereby confirmed either, and the checker established why: that result set was led by `paraform.com/blog/openai-forward-deployed-engineer` — *"What Is OpenAI's Forward Deployed Engineer? Role Breakdown, Skills & How to Hire One"* — and the prose returned is recruiter-blog register, not JD register. A domain-restricted follow-up asking for the posting's own criteria block returned *"the specific 'You might thrive in this role if you' criteria section from the job posting isn't fully visible in these search results."* **The repaired finding, which is stronger than either previous version: the OpenAI requirements block is not in the search index at all, and every years figure seen across beats 12–14 traces to third-party commentary.** Asserting the floor is an artefact is itself a claim about a document nobody has read — rule 11's sin pointed in the exculpatory direction. Both exits remain closed for the third beat: `Expired` requires the posting to have left the index and its canonical `openai.com` URL returned live in three separate sweeps this run; `Skipped` requires a non-requirements ground and Abu Dhabi sits inside the CV's "Open to relocation (UK / EU / Gulf)". PR #9 (`unreachable`) remains open and unmerged; the row is not downgraded pending review. Sponsorship: unverified. **— BEAT 15 UPDATE (2026-08-15): still `Re-evaluate`, fourth consecutive beat, and the ground is unchanged from beat 13's repair rather than re-argued.** The Indeed connector worked this beat, so the phrasings were re-run properly for the first time since beat 13. **Three phrasings, all recorded, none returning OpenAI:** `OpenAI forward deployed engineer` / Abu Dhabi → **0**; `OpenAI` / Abu Dhabi → 10 results (Riverflex, D4 Insight ×2, Acenet, Infinite pl, DICETEK ×3, Greenfield Marine), **no OpenAI**; `forward deployed software engineer` / Abu Dhabi → 5 (Palantir, Mistral, Gecko, Palantir Deployment Strategist, BRAIN), **no OpenAI**. **OpenAI has now never once appeared on the Indeed index in fifteen beats.** `openai.com` remains egress-blocked (`curl` → 000 this run, alongside google.com, jobs.lever.co and printerpix-2.betterteam.com; only `api.github.com` answered). Both exits stay closed for the same reasons beat 14 recorded and the checker re-affirmed: `Expired` requires the posting to have left the index, and nothing this beat shows that it has; `Skipped` requires a non-requirements ground, and Abu Dhabi sits inside the CV's "Open to relocation (UK / EU / Gulf)". Beat 14's repaired finding stands unamended — **the OpenAI requirements block is not in the search index at all, and every years figure seen across beats 12–15 traces to third-party commentary.** PR #9 (`unreachable`) remains open and unmerged; the row is not downgraded pending review. Sponsorship: unverified. **— BEAT 16 UPDATE (2026-08-16): still `Re-evaluate`, fifth consecutive beat, and nothing in the ground has moved.** `OpenAI` / Abu Dhabi → 10 results (Riverflex, DICETEK ×3, Infinite pl, D4 Insight ×2, Acenet, Greenfield Marine, Contango, Deeplight), **no OpenAI** — sixteen beats without a single appearance on the Indeed index. `openai.com` returned **000 to curl** this run. Both exits stay closed for the reasons beats 13–15 recorded and the checker re-affirmed: `Expired` requires the posting to have left the index and nothing shows that it has; `Skipped` requires a non-requirements ground, and Abu Dhabi sits inside the CV's "Open to relocation (UK / EU / Gulf)". Beat 14's repaired finding stands unamended — the OpenAI requirements block is not in the search index at all, and every years figure seen across beats 12–16 traces to third-party commentary. **PR #9 (`unreachable`) remains open and unmerged, and beat 16 proposes widening it: this row is no longer one hard case but one of five that share a single permanent, infrastructural blocker** — OpenAI (openai.com), Palantir (jobs.lever.co), Alkymi (linkedin.com) and both Printerpix rows (betterteam.com, glassdoor.com). The row is not downgraded pending review. Sponsorship: unverified. **— BEAT 17 UPDATE (2026-08-17): still `Re-evaluate`, held not downgraded, and the checker ruled explicitly that holding is correct rather than the routine hiding behind its own rule.** Its reasoning, which is worth keeping because it is the cleanest statement of why these rows persist: **an Indeed search is ranked retrieval, not enumeration, so absence from ten results is not absence from the index** — and `Expired` cannot be established at all while the host carrying the document is egress-blocked. Egress re-probed this run and unchanged: `curl` returned **000 for google.com, jobs.lever.co, openai.com, linkedin.com and printerpix-2.betterteam.com**, and **200 only for api.github.com**. Phrasing run and failed: `OpenAI` / Abu Dhabi → 10 results (Riverflex, VAM SYSTEMS, Infinite pl, DICETEK ×2, D4 Insight ×2, Acenet, Contango, Deeplight), **no OpenAI** — **seventeen beats without a single appearance on the Indeed index.** **THE CHECKER RECOMMENDED CLOSING THIS ROW AND THE DRAFT DECLINED TO ACT ON IT UNILATERALLY, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE RULE.** Its argument is strong and is recorded in full: seventeen beats of non-reproduction on the only index the routine can query means this is no longer a debt to re-evaluate but a **phantom**, it consumes a search every beat for nothing, and it is *"the 'a figure a second unprimed retrieval cannot reproduce is not a document' principle applied to a posting"* — so it should be closed as **never-established**, explicitly *not* `Expired`, which would assert it left the index. **But `never-established` is not in the verdict vocabulary, and the vocabulary permits exactly two exits from `re-evaluate`: a real verdict, or `Expired` — which is categorically unavailable here because the posting is live.** Inventing a third exit inside a beat to clear a queue is precisely what the debt rule forbids, and beat 13 already set the precedent for this situation by proposing `unreachable` **by PR** rather than adopting it. **The row therefore holds, and the closure is proposed by PR this beat instead.** Sponsorship: unverified. |

| 2026-08-13 | JOBSEARCH_1256 (checker's re-pull: `_1344`) | Partner Implementation Specialist I | TCP Software (TimeClock Plus) | Lahore | **Shortlisted** | **Delivered. The first keep to survive the checker since beat 5, and it survived on affirmative matches rather than on absences.** Found by the beat-11 varied-phrasing action applied to a city that phrasing had never been run against — `implementation specialist customer onboarding software` / **Lahore**. Zero prior "TCP Software" rows. JD pulled by the draft and **re-pulled independently by the checker**, which confirmed every quotation verbatim and confirmed standing decision 10 again inside one beat (`_1256` → `_1344`). **The combination that has not appeared in thirteen beats: an open experience band and no field-of-degree gate at the same time.** Beat 12's lane finding 2 concluded "the bar that blocks her domestically is not experience and has not been for several beats — it is the field of the degree"; CureMD, AIO App and Eposmatic each cleared the first and failed the second. This posting clears both. **Rule 6 opens twice over:** "Bachelor's degree in MIS **or equivalent work experience** preferred" — the "or equivalent" form is the Gecko side of beat 5's split (kept) against Brain Co.'s "or a related field" (dropped), i.e. the one verbal form beat 12's "softer is not open" ruling identifies as actually opening rule 6 — and the whole bullet is then graded "preferred" under standing decision 13. Rule 6's second half (the ContentStudio precedent, "the stack must match too") is vacuous here because **the Requirements block names no technology at all**; the only product named is "1–2 core TCP products", which the same bullet says she will *learn*. **That is what separates it from the two keeps that died:** Contour (`_1086`, beat 11) fell on three unqualified Required bullets naming Windows Server, strong Excel and CSV transformation; CareCloud (`_1087`, beat 12) fell on the field-of-degree form. Neither failure mode is present. **The five affirmative matches, each to a CV line:** "Strong written and verbal communication skills" → "stakeholder communication, runbooks and technical documentation", IELTS 7.0 full professional proficiency, Teacher 2023–2026, and Kay & A's "**client-facing administrative support**"; "Basic understanding of software platforms and data configuration" → "Node.js, REST APIs, Firebase (Auth, Firestore), **JSON Schema**" and "Docker, Vercel, Oracle Cloud (ARM64), Linux, Tailscale, CI/CD"; "Support customer and partner training through standard **virtual** sessions" → "Economics & Business Teacher... Designed curriculum and taught quantitative problem solving" (and the Progree internship is itself logged "Virtual"); "Maintain accurate project records and documentation" → "wrote a **seven-phase runbook that let another engineer reproduce it unaided**", plus Askari's "monthly-to-annual financial statements" and "fixed-asset registers with depreciation"; "Perform basic system configuration and setup **with guidance**" → "Deployed an n8n automation stack on ARM64 Linux with Docker, fronted by Tailscale Funnel for stable HTTPS without a public IP". **Rationale corrected by the checker on the load-bearing bullet, and the correction matters.** The draft mapped "**0–1 years** of experience in a customer support or technical onboarding role" to MB Dev Studio's "post-launch support". The checker struck it: a founder-led studio three months old naming **zero clients** is not "a customer support or technical onboarding role", and asserting it edges toward rule 7. **The bullet is satisfied on its face because the stated band includes zero** — beat 12 already recorded CureMD's "0–1 year" and AIO App's "0–2 years" as genuinely open bars — and the honest adjacent evidence is Kay & A (Oct 2006 – Feb 2007), which is dated, employed and client-facing. The stretch was unnecessary and is deleted. **Second checker addition, fit-favourable and omitted by the draft: the product domain is payroll.** TCP sells "timekeeping, scheduling, and **leave management**" and this seat handles "**PEO** client accounts" — a PEO is a payroll-outsourcing structure — against Askari's "**payroll and tax deductions**". Not a required bullet and not dressed as one, but real, dated and on the CV. **Seniority language points down, not up** (rule 11's corollary, inverted): compensation field is empty, and the body reads "Perform basic system configuration and setup **with guidance**", "**Escalate** complex integration or partner-specific issues **to senior team members**", "**Build foundational knowledge**", "**Assist in** implementing". **Frictions disclosed, neither a gate.** (1) **Lahore, not Islamabad** — the fact sheet says "a Lahore posting is a **relocation call**, not a commute", assigning the decision to the candidate; the checker read the posting end to end and confirmed **no onsite requirement, no residency clause, no shift clause**, the direct contrast being Podium below, which states both. Honest caveat the checker added: the CV header offers relocation to **UK / EU / Gulf** only, so domestic relocation is not advertised on the document itself. (2) Rule 15's corollary was tested and does not bite — location is not a graded clause of this JD, it is not a clause of this JD at all. Sponsorship: **no** (stated) — "we are not able to sponsor visas for this role" — with no practical effect on a domestic seat for a Pakistan-based candidate, and **scoped "for this role"**, so it is not employer-wide intelligence under standing decision 14's corollary. |
| 2026-08-13 | JOBSEARCH_1399 | AI Full-Stack & Automation Engineer | COLOR STUDIO PROFESSIONAL | Korangi, Karachi (in person) | reopen-when-flagship-ships | **The densest artifact-level match in the log, and it fails on exactly one thing.** Found on a Lane B axis re-run against a Lane A city (`technical implementation engineer customer deployment` / Karachi), an axis pairing never tried before. New employer, posted 2026-08-08, Rs100,000–150,000. **No degree requirement anywhere, no years bar anywhere**, and hiring is portfolio-first — the application asks for "GitHub profile, Portfolio, Links to live products you personally built, Links to any published iOS/Android applications... How you currently use **Claude Code**, Cursor, ChatGPT or other coding agents". **The met half, line for line against shipped artifacts:** the mobile block names "React Native / Expo / TypeScript / Authentication / **Subscriptions** / **In-app purchases** / **Google Play deployment**" against OpenClaw Prep, which is React Native · Expo (EAS) · TypeScript · Firebase Auth · offline-first sync · **in-app purchases** · "Release build in closed testing on **Google Play Console**"; automation names "**Playwright**, Browser automation, ... **n8n**", both shipped; AI-assisted development names "**MCP tools**" and Claude Code, and MCP is a fact-sheet line with an artifact behind it; cloud names "**Vercel** ... **Docker**, GitHub, GitHub Actions, **CI/CD**", all on the skills line. **It drops on the JD's explicit "What We Are NOT Looking For" block** — "**Cannot design a backend independently**", "**Cannot design a database**", "Has only frontend experience" — against fact-sheet line 38 (Node.js is a skills-line keyword with **no shipped standalone Node backend**) and the datastore position (Firestore is the only datastore on record; no PostgreSQL, no SQL). **The checker's ruling, and it sets a precedent the log did not carry: an explicit "What We Are NOT Looking For" list is the *strongest* requirement form in a JD, binding harder than a Required bullet** — a Required bullet states what the employer wants, an exclusion bullet states the filter used to reject, and standing decision 13's "the verb decides" test finds no softening verb at all in "Cannot design a backend independently". Rule 15's corollary is then dispositive: the draft conceded the clause and shortlisted anyway, which is the beat-4 Linux Recruit pattern for the third consecutive beat. **On whether the density of the met half changes it, the checker said no, and located why:** the met items sit in the Mobile, Automation, AI-assisted-development and Cloud blocks, while the Full-Stack, Backend & Data and AI blocks name Node.js, PostgreSQL, SQL, relational schemas, queues, data pipelines, caching, search, "**OpenAI APIs, Anthropic / Claude APIs**", embeddings and vector search — a near-complete enumeration of the fact sheet's named gaps — plus a Shopify ecosystem absent from the CV entirely. "This is not 'mostly met with two gaps'; it is two halves and she owns one." **Logged `reopen-when-flagship-ships` rather than `Skipped`, on the checker's recommendation, and it is the first time that verdict has been clearly earned rather than decorative:** everything failing is one thing — a backend with a relational schema — and the fact sheet's pending-evidence line exists for exactly this. **Reopen condition: the flagship ships a Node/Postgres backend.** Also "Work Location: In person", Korangi. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-13 | JOBSEARCH_1232 (checker's re-pull: `_1398`) | Deployment Strategist | Palantir Technologies | Abu Dhabi | Skipped | **A new title + company pair at a core Lane B target — the log carried Palantir *FDSE* rows only, so standing decision 10 did not cover it and it had never been judged.** JD pulled by the draft and re-pulled by the checker. Its entire "What We Value" block: "Extraordinary ability to take on open-ended problems in unstructured environments" · "Adaptive and introspective; willing to learn, teach, lead and follow" · "Ability to travel 25 – 75% **required**" · "Experience with programming, scripting or statistical packages (e.g. Python, R, Matlab, SQL) **is a plus**". **No degree requirement, no years bar, no required stack** — materially different from the Palantir FDSE clause ("Strong engineering background, preferred in fields such as Computer Science...") that founded standing decision 13. **Verdict upheld, ground overturned, and the ruling is the finding of the beat.** The draft dropped it on rule 11 (argument from silence, the Linux Recruit `_193` pattern). **The checker refused that ground and ruled that responsibilities blocks *are* affirmative asks under rule 11:** the rule says "name the fact-sheet line **the posting** affirmatively asks for" — *the posting*, not the requirements block — and standing decision 3 exists precisely because the whole JD is the evidence. Matching a fact-sheet line to a responsibility is an affirmative argument; absence is the only thing rule 11 forbids. **But responsibilities defeat rule 11's main clause only and cannot carry a keep** — they answer "is there anything affirmative here at all?", not rule 4 or rule 11's corollary. That is the beat-11 Rayyanco move exactly: refuse rule 11 as the ground, then rebuild. **The rebuilt ground is rule 4 and lane, and the JD draws the line itself:** "Identify relevant datasets... and **work with Forward Deployed Engineers** to integrate the data into a stable and extensible pipeline" — the posting places the FDE as a different person on the same team, and the target role is a client-facing *delivery engineer*. **The sharp version, which is beat 12's CareCloud finding inverted: a seat that grades programming "is a plus" is a seat where her single differentiated asset is worth the least.** The clause reads as widening and in fact narrows the contest to the other three bullets — one partial match ("willing to learn, **teach**, lead and follow" → Teacher 2023–2026), one working-conditions clause, and one unevidenced trait assertion ("**Extraordinary** ability..."), the same intensifier shape as the "**extensive** experience" clause beat 12 rebuilt the Linux Recruit drop on. **The checker explicitly declined the draft's alternative ground "Palantir's known hiring bar" — that is reputation, not the JD, and standing decision 3 forbids it.** *Fit-favourable and genuinely strong rather than decorative:* "**Lead training sessions** to ensure that the product is meeting user needs"; "**Present the results of our work**... to audiences ranging from analysts to **C-suite executives**"; "Go onsite and meet with customer analysts to **understand the critical questions they need to answer** and locate their biggest pain points"; "the desire to **dive into the details of the data**" — against MSc Economics & Finance (Distinction, CGPA 3.92) and Askari's audit/reconciliation/financial-statement line. **Employer intelligence: Palantir's Deployment Strategist ladder is the least-gated Palantir req in the log — no degree clause at all — and the one Palantir ladder where her non-engineering half is the asset rather than the liability.** Watch list, Okta-style. Sponsorship: unverified (checker read the full posting; note also "you must work from the city and or country in which you are employed"). |

Riyadh is inside the CV's Gulf target, **no Arabic gate appears**, and "Relevant certifications (e.g., CISSP, CompTIA Security+, CEH) **are a plus**" — graded, so their absence is not a ground. Two different documents behind one URL is beat 13's *"two different strings for one bullet"* finding arriving at the level of the whole posting, so **no verdict is reachable and the debt is honest.** **The fit-favourable reading was tested and it collapses**, which matters because a checker that only attacks keeps does half the job: the retrieved MCP clause is *"**eagerness to learn and adopt** Model Context Protocol (MCP) concepts"* and *"**learning and working with** proprietary MCP implementations"*. Standing decision 13's "the verb decides" test puts "eagerness to learn" below even Gecko's kept "*familiarity with*" — the posting asks for no MCP experience, so the CV's *"Playwright regression suite ... exposed to agents over MCP"* has nothing to attach to, and building a shortlist on the rare keyword is standing decision 15 exactly. Under the other rendering the ask is affirmative and adverse: *"knowledge of vector databases (Pinecone, Weaviate, FAISS)"* against the fact sheet's named vector-DB gap. **Both renderings land outside a shortlist by different routes; the near-miss does not exist.** Sponsorship: unknown — still not read. **— BEAT 15 UPDATE (2026-08-15): the debt is CLEARED, and it is cleared on a first-party JD rather than on a rendering.** The Indeed connector loaded this beat. `Printerpix` / Dubai returned the req under this exact title and company as `JOBSEARCH_1408` (posted 24 Jul 2026) and `get_job_details` returned the full body, so standing decision 3 is satisfied for the first time in three beats. **Verdict: `Skipped`, and the drop is over-determined.** "**2-5 years of hands-on experience in software engineering, machine learning, or AI systems development**" is rule 2's drop condition against *no paid employment as a software engineer*. The technical block is then the fact sheet's named gaps read back almost in order: "**Expert-level proficiency in Python**" (the sheet's Python is real but lighter than TypeScript, with no backend on record), "**AWS Expertise**: EC2, Lambda, S3, RDS, **EKS**, SageMaker", "**FastAPI Development**" (the named missing Python backend, by name), RAG pipelines, LangChain/LlamaIndex/CrewAI, distributed systems "handling thousands of concurrent interactions", with vector databases (Pinecone/Weaviate/Milvus) and **Kubernetes** in Nice-to-Have. Not one of the fact sheet's shipped artifacts attaches to a required clause. **The checker could NOT ratify this clearance and that is recorded rather than smoothed over:** the shared Indeed quota was exhausted before it could re-pull, so the quotations above are the draft's alone. **It did, however, refute the draft's headline claim, and the refutation is the more useful finding.** The draft proposed that this first-party JD settled the two-beat MCP dispute by containing no MCP mention. It does not settle it: the checker ran an MCP-unprimed query against the `printerpix-2.betterteam.com/ai-agent-systems-engineer-1` slug and found it indexed under the title "**Junior AI Engineer, Dubai**" with a *different* requirements text — LangChain/CrewAI/LangGraph, LLM APIs with structured output parsing, RAG, marketing-platform API integrations, and "**Nice to have skills include MCP (Model Context Protocol)**". Two different documents, so the most likely reading is beat 14's original one: **Printerpix runs more than one AI req, and the betterteam slug is not this posting.** The dispute is reopened, not closed. It changes no verdict in either direction — "Nice to have" sits below Gecko's kept "familiarity with" (standing decision 13), so beat 14's collapse of the MCP near-miss holds on the betterteam text, while this Indeed text drops on years and expert-level Python. **Both routes land outside a shortlist; the near-miss still does not exist.** Sponsorship: unverified (the JD states none). |

Sponsorship: unknown. **— BEAT 15 UPDATE (2026-08-15): still `Re-evaluate`, `Expired` is unavailable, and the draft's evidence for closing it was struck by the checker.** The draft argued toward `Expired` on the ground that `Printerpix` / Dubai now returns exactly four reqs (`_1408` AI Agent Systems Engineer - Mid Level, `_1409` AI Creative Specialist, `_1410` Talent Acquisition & HR Specialist, `_1411` Senior Management Accountant) and this title is not among them. **The checker refuted the inference and demonstrated the defect in-run on an unrelated posting: an employer-name query returning N reqs without title X is not evidence that X is absent from the index.** Its demonstration: `MILES Mobility` / Berlin → 2 results, not including MILES's own live Chatbot Specialist req; `Chatbot Specialist` / Berlin → **0**, the posting's own exact title returning nothing; `Chatbot` / Berlin → 10 results with that same posting **first**. Two of three queries that should have found a demonstrably indexed posting returned nothing. The Printerpix evidence is the same query shape and inherits the same defect. **Second and independent ground, which decides the count:** this row was created on **beat 14, the beat that ran zero Indeed queries because the connector was absent**. `expired` means *"the posting left the **index**"* — beat 14's failure was an egress block on a host, not an absence from an index nobody could query, and **a beat with no index access cannot be counted as a beat of failed retrieval.** Counting it would let a tooling outage manufacture an `expired` verdict, which is precisely the queue-clearing the vocabulary forbids. **This beat is the first, not the second.** The draft also asked whether the first-party `_1408` JD resolves this row as a rename; **the checker ruled it an inference too far, and its own evidence cuts against it** — the betterteam slug is indexed under *this* row's title with the junior/marketing agent-framework requirements shape, not the mid-level systems shape of `_1408`. Folding this row into that one would bury a posting nobody has read. Seen, never judged. Sponsorship: unknown. **— BEAT 16 UPDATE (2026-08-16): still `Re-evaluate`, and the draft's proposal to mark it `Expired` was OVERTURNED BY THE CHECKER ON FIRST-PARTY EVIDENCE.** The draft argued `Expired` was now formally available: beat 15 ruled itself the first beat of failed retrieval, this beat is the second, the employer is demonstrably still listing (`Printerpix` / Dubai → the same 4 reqs), and two further phrasings failed this run (`Junior AI Engineer Marketing` / Dubai → 4, no Printerpix junior AI req; `AI workflow operations engineer` / Dubai → 10, Printerpix present only as *AI Agent Systems Engineer*). **The checker added two phrasings of its own — `Junior AI Engineer` / Dubai → 10 (Printerpix present only as AI Creative Specialist, `_1699`) and `AI Operations Engineer` / Dubai → 10 (no Printerpix at all) — so four phrasings failed this beat, and then it went off-connector and found the posting live.** `printerpix-2.betterteam.com/ai-agent-systems-engineer-1` returns as a first-class search result under the current title **"Junior AI Engineer, Dubai"**. Beat 13 settled this question against OpenAI and beat 15 against Palantir and Alkymi: **a posting demonstrably live cannot be `Expired` whatever the retrieval count.** `expired` is *"a fact about the posting"*, and the fact is that it has not gone anywhere. The two-beat count is a necessary condition, not a sufficient one, and the liveness condition fails first. **Two findings that make the draft's case worse rather than better.** First, the beat-15 MILES retrieval defect was **reproduced on this exact row**: one checker search returned `ae.indeed.com/viewjob?jk=10a5682f13ffd475` titled "Junior AI Engineer - Dubai", so the req may be *in the Indeed index* while the connector has failed to surface it across five phrasings and two beats — the draft's evidence is not evidence of absence, it is a second demonstration of the defect. Second, and conceptually underneath it: **this row was never sourced from the Indeed index at all.** It was found by WebSearch on betterteam. Failing to retrieve from Indeed a posting that was never on Indeed is a category error, and expiring on that basis would be inventing the verdict to clear the queue — the one thing the vocabulary says `expired` must never be used for. **The draft's best argument was that the error is cheap, since an `expired` row is never deduped and would be re-judged on re-listing; the checker rejected it, and the rejection is the durable finding: "the error is cheap" is not a reason to record something false.** Doing so would put a demonstrable falsehood in a log whose whole value is that it is trustworthy, and would set the precedent that connector silence retires a debt — the precedent most likely to bury something real, given that a connector false negative was just reproduced on this very posting. **Evidence retrieved this beat and deliberately NOT converted into a verdict** (third-party synthesis of an egress-blocked host — beat 14's blending hazard, the same discipline beat 15 applied to the Palantir criteria block): the page reads entry-to-junior, AED 4,500–5,500/month, asking a *"strong foundation in **Python** programming, AI integrations, data processing, and **machine learning concepts**"*, with **Model Context Protocol** named in the company blurb — MCP is on the fact sheet, ML concepts are not. The positive explanation for the retrieval failure also stands unchanged and is the reason the count is beside the point: **these rows were never sourced from the Indeed index at all**, so Indeed silence is not evidence about them.
